FOREWORD 

The preparation and prosecution of an application for patent to the end that the issued patent will adequately protect the particular invention are undertakings which require knowledge of patent law and Patent Office practice, as well as knowledge of the technical aspects of the invention. For this reason, most inventors employ the services of registered patent attorneys or agents to do the work for them. 

The Patent Office maintains a register of attorneys and agents who have demonstrated their qualifications and are recognized to practice before the Office. Persons who are not recognized by the Patent Office for this practice are not permitted by law to represent inventors. Those registered as "patent attorneys" are attorneys-at-law, except for some non-lawyers who were registered prior to 1938. Qualified persons who are not attorneys-at-law may be registered as patent agents. Firms of patent attorneys and patent agents may also be registered. 

In addition to the present publication, the Patent Office publishes a complete list of all persons and firms registered to practice entitled "Roster of Attorneys and 

Agents Registered to Practice Before the U. S. Patent Office," available from the Superintendent of Documents, U. S. Government Printing Office, Washington 25, 

D. C., at a cost of $1.00. A number of those listed in the Roster are not, by reason of their association with their employers, available to represent new clients. 

The present publication, "Patent Attorneys and Agents Available to Represent Inventors Before the United States Patent Office," lists those patent attorneys and agents who are available to accept employment by inventors. The list includes all registrants who have not informed the Patent Office that they are employees of the United States Government or full-time employees of corporations. The list also includes the names of those who are full-time employees of corporations but have stated that they are, nevertheless, free to represent clients other than their employers. The corporate employer of each of those in the latter category is identified in the listing.

The listing is arranged alphabetically by states and cities within states where the registrants have offices. Firms are listed together with individuals. Registrants having offices other than in the United States are listed in a separate following section, the countries and cities being arranged alphabetically.
